On a NX100 with 20-50mm lens fitted, upgrading from 1.00 to 1.10 the following was observed.

- This doesn't follow the normal protocol rules.
- First Byte / Five Bytes are not the length of the message. 
- The first message is probably putting the lens in a special mode. Perhaps a high bit in the first byte is special.
- The lens response is always 02 10 00 92 80
- The third byte is a counter. Probably a 4-byte header , 2048 bytes data, 1 byte checksum?
- The counter goes up 1024 bytes at a time. Is it 16-bit words, or maybe the data has redundancy.
- The lens gets reset at the end. Perhaps that is how it  gets back to the normal protocol mode.
- In the firmware binary file, every 4th byte is zero.
- In the data every 4th byte is 0 or 0x80.
- There are many repeating 4-byte sequences. Perhaps every 4 bytes of data represents 2 bytes of firmware. Actually there are many much longer repeating sequences, found a 60 byte sequence that repeats a couple of times.
- There is much more data in the firmware file than is transmitted to the lens.
- The data sent to the lens is not obviously direct from the file. It must either be encrypted before sending to the lens, or more likely its encrypted in the file, and gets decrypted before sending?

&nbsp;

Doesn't help to fill in any of the protocol blanks, so will leave it here.
